#2059. 26-year-old’s absence for Millwall in QPR defeat hints at 2027 exit: Opinion

Millwall suffered their third consecutive defeat in the Championship last weekend as they lost 1-0 to QPR. The Lions went down to 10 men after 15 minutes when Zak Sturge was sent off for denying a goalscoring opportunity.

Alex Neil had the option to send Alfie Doughty on to compensate for the dismissal. However, he opted to use midfielder Camiel Neghli at left-back for the rest of the first half instead, before right-back Jordi Osei-Tutu was put there for the final 45 minutes.

Millwall signed Doughty from Luton Town in the 2025 summer transfer window. It has been a disastrous spell at The Den for the 26-year-old, and questions are rising about his future for the Lions.

Injuries have been a major problem for the South London outfit for two seasons running now, and Doughty is one of several extreme cases. He made 28 appearances for Millwall last term but has only featured four times for Neil’s side this campaign.

The 26-year-old has started just 17 games for the Lions. The left-back was arguably Luton Town’s best player in the Premier League during the 2023/24 season, where he grabbed two goals and provided eight assists. However, he now looks far from that quality.

Doughty’s lack of involvement under Neil suggests that he isn’t trusted to play for Millwall, even when the South London outfit could’ve used his attacking quality. It was a great opportunity against QPR to prove his worth to the Lions’ head coach, but he was second choice to players in other positions.

There is still time for the 26-year-old to turn around his career at The Den. Obviously, his 49 minutes this season suggest it’s too little too late for the left-back. However, with Sturge suspended, Neil could give him one more chance before the new year.

Doughty has shown his quality on occasions for Millwall. Last campaign, he grabbed five assists from an xA of 3.76. He also had a successful cross rate of 40.4%. However, the matter of fact is that the former Luton Town man hasn’t shown his talent enough to warrant much of a future for the South London outfit.

Realistically, the Lions must decide whether he is worth keeping for the 2027/28 term. The 26-year-old’s transfer value will have dropped considerably. Nonetheless, Neil’s side won’t want to lose him for nothing regardless of his lack of availability.

#2058. B4Love, la Serie BKT in campo contro la violenza sulle donne

La Serie BKT dice no alla violenza sulle donne. In occasione della 13a giornata di campionato, in programma tra il 24 e il 25 novembre, in concomitanza con la Giornata internazionale per l’eliminazione della violenza contro le donne, la Lega Serie B rinnova il proprio impegno sul fronte della responsabilità sociale con “B4Love”, la campagna di sensibilizzazione che invita a promuovere valori di uguaglianza e inclusione a discapito di qualsiasi discriminazione.

“La violenza non fa parte del gioco. In campo e nella vita scegliamo il rispetto”: questo il claim che accompagnerà le iniziative del prossimo turno, con l'obiettivo di utilizzare la visibilità del campionato e la forza del calcio per contribuire a diffondere consapevolezza su un tema che riguarda l'intera società.

Su tutti i campi della Serie BKT tornerà protagonista l'iconico Pallone Rosso, emblema della campagna e realizzato da Kappa®, che dal 2021/2022 accompagna la Lega nelle progettualità che la vedono impegnata nel contrasto alla violenza sulle donne.

“Il calcio ha una responsabilità che non si limita al mero risultato sportivo – evidenzia il Presidente della Lega Serie B Paolo Bedin -. Con B4Love intendiamo ribadire fermamente che il rispetto deve essere un principio irrinunciabile, dentro e fuori dal campo, e che contrastare ogni forma di violenza costituisce una responsabilità collettiva. Un messaggio che trova il suo simbolo più immediato e riconoscibile nel Pallone Rosso, per il quale ringrazio Kappa® della collaborazione solida e duratura. Questo impegno si inserisce nel percorso più ampio che abbiamo avviato con B4People Impresa Sociale, la Piattaforma della Lega dedicata alla Sostenibilità Sociale e Ambientale, attraverso la quale vogliamo dare organicità, continuità e concretezza alle nostre iniziative di responsabilità sociale. L’obiettivo è creare una sinergia positiva tra la Lega, le Società e i territori che rappresentiamo per prevenire le discriminazioni di genere e gli abusi, a beneficio della tutela delle donne e della loro dignità”.

“Da sei stagioni portiamo in campo il Pallone Rosso insieme alla Lega Serie B. Per noi di Kappa® è un modo per dare, attraverso il calcio, un segnale forte e concreto contro la violenza sulle donne e promuovere un messaggio di sensibilizzazione e responsabilità condivisa. Siamo orgogliosi di rinnovare questo gesto e ringraziamo il presidente Paolo Bedin e tutta la Lega per la collaborazione” dichiara Alessandro Boglione, CEO del Gruppo BasicNet.

Per l’occasione, sono previste anche diverse attivazioni: durante il cerimoniale pre-gara, i calciatori e gli ufficiali di gara entreranno in campo accompagnati da ragazze, mentre i capitani indosseranno una fascia appositamente realizzata per B4Love. Le divise di gioco saranno inoltre arricchite dalla patch dedicata alla campagna, oltre alla presenza di visibilità led bordocampo e sui maxischermi, come anche messaggi speaker e una corposa campagna di comunicazione sui canali ufficiali della Lega Serie B, di Kappa® e delle Società della Serie BKT per amplificare la portata dell’iniziativa contro qualsiasi forma di prevaricazione.

La Lega Serie B, attraverso B4People Impresa Sociale e la campagna B4Love, conferma la volontà di mettere il calcio al servizio di valori sani per un impegno collettivo strutturato. Perché il rispetto non è soltanto una regola dello sport, ma un principio fondamentale da affermare sempre in ogni contesto.

#2056. Sinner shock, operazione al ginocchio: rientra in primavera

Il tennista sarà operato a Turku il 24 novembre; le prime stime indicano un recupero di circa tre-quattro mesi.

Jannik Sinner ha deciso di sottoporsi a un intervento al ginocchio destro. L’operazione è programmata per martedì 24 novembre a Turku, in Finlandia, e sarà eseguita dal professor Lasse Lempainen. La scelta arriva dopo diversi consulti medici e oltre quattro mesi di terapie conservative che non hanno dato i risultati sperati.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Le prime stime indicano un recupero di circa tre-quattro mesi. Il rientro potrebbe avvenire in primavera, ma i tempi dipenderanno dall’esito dell’intervento e dalla successiva riabilitazione.

Il 12 luglio Sinner aveva conquistato il suo secondo Wimbledon consecutivo battendo Alexander Zverev in finale. Quella partita è stata anche la sua ultima apparizione ufficiale della stagione. Nelle settimane successive il tennista italiano ha cominciato ad avvertire dolore al ginocchio destro.

Il problema è stato identificato come una sindrome della bandelletta ileotibiale, che provoca dolore nella parte esterna dell’articolazione. Per un atleta sottoposto a sollecitazioni continue può diventare particolarmente fastidiosa.

All’inizio Sinner e il suo gruppo di lavoro avevano scelto di evitare la chirurgia. Il percorso prevedeva riposo, fisioterapia e trattamenti specifici, con l’obiettivo di recuperare gradualmente la piena funzionalità del ginocchio senza correre rischi inutili o compromettere ulteriormente la carriera del tennista.

Nonostante i tentativi e i consulti con diversi specialisti, il dolore non è scomparso definitivamente. Il numero due del mondo ha quindi preso in considerazione l’intervento. Decisivo è stato l’ultimo confronto con Lempainen, chirurgo ortopedico finlandese specializzato nel trattamento delle lesioni muscolari e tendinee degli sportivi professionisti.

Tra gli atleti già seguiti dal medico figurano anche i calciatori Ousmane Dembélé e Leonardo Spinazzola. Dopo la valutazione della situazione clinica di Sinner, la scelta è ricaduta sull’operazione a Turku.

Burnley came within 15 minutes of their first win since October 2025, before Todd Cantwell levelled for Blackburn Rovers as the East Lancashire derby ended 2-2. Fabio Celestini is yet to win any of his first four games in charge, drawing three and losing one.

There’s been a lot of problems for the Clarets this season, but none more so than their defence. No team in the Championship has conceded more than their 33 goals, with an unsettling goalkeeper situation and a lack of defensive leadership and focus meaning Celestini still has a lot of work to do.

Chris Davies‘ Birmingham City head into Wednesday night’s game looking to hit back from a 1-0 defeat to 10-man Stoke City. The loss saw the Potters leapfrog BCFC, but they know a win could guide them to top spot if other results go their way.

To get back to the top of the tree, Davies will have to reignite his side’s attack. Birmingham City had scored eight in their last three games prior to the Stoke City loss, but could not convert in the 90 minutes. Jay Stansfield has been in fine form, and August Priske is fit again after his spell on the sidelines.

“Burnley’s struggles are showing no signs of stopping. For much of their derby clash with Blackburn Rovers, they looked like they could have killed off the game but instead let their opposition back in.

“BCFC’s loss to Stoke City feels like a simple bump in the road for Chris Davies’ side. When you hit a bump, you need to face a team out of sorts, and that’s exactly what Birmingham City have in front of them. With this team’s scoring power against Burnley’s defence, there looks only one outcome.

“I can only see the Burnley misery continuing as the Blues return to form.”

They’re so poor at the back though – there’s a serious lack of awareness and near non-existent game management, and it means they’re deservedly in a dismal position.

“Birmingham City can definitely punish the hosts for another poor display. BCFC will be determined to bounce back after their impressive run was slowed with a defeat at Stoke City, and they should be confident of getting back to winning ways.

“The Blues are going to be without suspended captain Christoph Klarer, but I can’t see that being an issue in truth. I’m going for an away win as Burnley hit establish a new low with a 24th home game without victory.”

#2052. Lazio, Gattuso e il mercato: Isaksen, Noslin e Taylor vogliono restare

Il tecnico ha parlato con diversi giocatori; intanto il Brentford corteggia Isaksen e resta il rischio di un mercato bloccato.

Isaksen, Noslin e Taylor vogliono restare alla Lazio nonostante le attenzioni del mercato. Secondo quanto riportato da Corriere dello Sport, Gattuso ha parlato nelle ultime settimane con diversi giocatori per conoscerne gli umori e, finora, ha raccolto soltanto segnali favorevoli alla permanenza. Il tecnico spera che la sessione di gennaio non rompa il legame costruito con la squadra.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Gattuso ha descritto così il rapporto nato nel gruppo: «Mi prendo un merito, aver fatto diventare questa squadra una famiglia». Nel parlare di famiglia ha richiamato anche il «senso di appartenenza» dei giocatori. È su questo legame che punta per trattenere i protagonisti della squadra, mentre cominciano a circolare le prime voci di mercato.

Il timore è quello di un nuovo ridimensionamento dopo le partenze iniziate un anno fa con Guendouzi e Castellanos e proseguite con altri giocatori, giustificate con la formula «hanno chiesto di andare via». Gattuso ha cercato di coinvolgere tutti, dando spazio anche a chi in precedenza si sentiva fuori posto. Il gruppo viene descritto attraverso i sorrisi, le parole e il clima tra i calciatori, ora al primo posto. Ai giocatori è attribuita una presa di posizione collettiva: «Siamo con Rino. Lottiamo per lui».

Nel rapporto con la squadra pesano anche la fame maturata dopo Zenica e l’idea di calcio affinata dal tecnico negli anni. Il modello della Lazio come gruppo familiare viene richiamato attraverso la storia del club, a partire dal primo scudetto. Sabato, all’Olimpico, i tifosi hanno cantato il nome di Gattuso.

Da giorni circolano interessamenti per gli olandesi Taylor e Noslin e per i danesi Isaksen e Provstgaard. Isaksen è stato tra i primi a esprimersi pubblicamente sul proprio futuro. Il Brentford di Matthew Benham lo corteggia: l’imprenditore britannico è noto per il lavoro svolto con Brentford e Midtjylland, il club nel quale l’esterno danese è emerso. Benham conosce bene il calcio danese.

Alla prospettiva della Premier League, Isaksen ha risposto: «La Premier? Voglio la Champions con la Lazio». Anche Provstgaard piace da tempo in Inghilterra. Taylor e Noslin possono attirare offerte, mentre Frattesi ha rifiutato alcune opzioni sia a gennaio sia a luglio.

Finora nessuno dei giocatori ha manifestato a Gattuso la volontà di partire. Da Taylor a Noslin, da Isaksen a Frattesi, prevale l’intenzione di proseguire il percorso con la Lazio. Le posizioni espresse dai calciatori chiamano la società a rispettare il patto costruito tra allenatore e squadra.

#2049. Il 2007 conquista la scena. Mora secondo solo a Yamal per numeri nei 5 migliori campionati

Messo un po' in secondo piano a causa del rendimento stratosferico di Donyell Malen, Rodrigo Mora sta costruendo una storia tutta sua. Il talento portoghese, appena 19 anni, si è preso un ruolo sempre più importante nella Roma di Gian Piero Gasperini, dimostrando di essere molto più di un investimento per il futuro. A Parma è arrivato il quarto gol consecutivo in campionato, il quinto complessivo della sua prima stagione in giallorosso.

L'inizio nella Capitale non era stato semplice. Qualche panchina, ingressi dalla panchina e prestazioni ancora lontane dal suo potenziale avevano alimentato qualche dubbio sull'adattamento del giovane portoghese. Il passaggio a una squadra ambiziosa e le richieste tattiche di Gasperini richiedevano tempo, ma l'allenatore ha sempre creduto nelle sue qualità: "Non credo che oggi nel calcio ci sia un giovane capace di incidere come lui", aveva detto il tecnico a settembre, quando i segnali erano ancora meno evidenti. Oggi i numeri gli stanno dando ragione: Mora, riporta Tuttosport, è il secondo centrocampista della Serie A per gol ogni 90 minuti, con una media di 0,91, alle spalle soltanto di Cissé. Tra i giocatori nati dal 2007 in poi nei cinque principali campionati europei, in termini realizzativi, ha fatto meglio solo un certo Lamine Yamal.

Nell'era dei tre punti, prima dei vent'anni soltanto Pato e Kean erano riusciti a segnare in quattro presenze consecutive in Serie A. Un rendimento che gli è valso anche la convocazione con la nazionale maggiore portoghese, dove ha preso - numericamente - il posto di Cristiano Ronaldo. La Roma si gode così un investimento da 50 milioni complessivi, tra parte fissa e bonus. Malen resta il protagonista principale, ma Mora sta dimostrando di poter diventare uno dei volti del futuro giallorosso. Il talento c’è, la personalità anche: il classe 2007 sembra già pronto per un palcoscenico più grande della sua età.

#2048. Totti a Belve, in difficoltà con gli acronimi: "LGBTQIA+? Non sono tecnologico"

Nell’anticipazione dell’intervista, Francesca Fagnani suggerisce la prima parola della sigla latina.

Francesco Totti è alle prese con gli acronimi SPQR e LGBTQIA+ in un’anticipazione della sua intervista a Belve. L’ex capitano della Roma fatica a ricordare il significato della prima sigla e risponde con alcune battute alle domande di Francesca Fagnani sulla seconda. Lo scambio è riportato da Cinemaserietv.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Tra le prime domande dell’anteprima c’è quella su SPQR, l’acronimo latino che compare da secoli sullo stemma di Roma e che per anni è stato presente anche sulle maglie giallorosse. Totti non sembra ricordarne con precisione il significato: «SPQR erano quelle… che era… ‘na cosa che nun me ricordo bene».

Fagnani gli suggerisce la prima parola, Senatus. L’ex numero dieci prova a proseguire, poi si ferma: «Senatus? Mo’ pure il latino vuoi… Senatus Popolusco e Romanus so’ troppe frasi».

La conduttrice passa quindi a LGBTQIA+. Totti sembra non riconoscere l’acronimo e, inizialmente, lo associa alla tecnologia: «LGBT? Nun so’ tecnologico io. LGBTQ Plus. Ma che è, un telefonino?».

Fagnani insiste con la domanda e l’ex calciatore continua sullo stesso tono ironico: «18 Plus? Ma ndo’ le tiri fuori ‘ste domande?. Ma come famo?». Il botta e risposta fa parte delle anticipazioni dell’intervista che Totti concederà alla conduttrice.

Adesso sembra quasi impossibile la rincorsa del PSG alla terza Champions della sua storia, senza due titolari di questo livello, perché parliamo di due elementi fondamentali nelle ultime finali vinte dal PSG. Doué e Kvaratskhelia garantivano imprevedibilità, accelerazioni e qualità nell’uno contro uno: caratteristiche difficili da replicare anche per una rosa profonda come quella parigina.

Le alternative in rosa sono Mika Godts, Maghnes Akliouche e Ferran Torres, ma la differenza rispetto ai due titolari è sostanziale. Il PSG dovrà quindi affidarsi alla forza del gruppo e alla capacità di Luis Enrique di trovare nuove soluzioni.

#2045. 19-year-old Wolves star has to kick-on after Charlton Athletic match-winning performance: Opinion

Wolves have started the season very well and are just one point off top spot, currently sitting in 3rd. However, despite that start, a player expected to be a driving force for Cesar Peixoto’s side this season has struggled.

That man is Mateus Mane and he finally delivered the kind of performance Wolves supporters have been waiting for against Charlton Athletic, demonstrating exactly why the club continue to place so much faith in the highly rated teenager.

The 19-year-old provided two assists in a 2-1 victory at Molineux on Saturday, helping Peixoto’s side extend their unbeaten run to seven matches. It was a particularly encouraging afternoon for a player who has endured some frustrating moments during his first full campaign of regular senior involvement.

Mane missed a glorious opportunity in the first half, blazing over from close range after Kieran Trippier’s cross, but responded emphatically after the interval. Within three minutes of the restart, he had beaten his marker before delivering a driven cross for Adam Armstrong to open the scoring. He then used his strength to get beyond Amari’i Bell and square the ball for Rafiki Said to register his first Wolves goal.

There is plenty of evidence that Mane’s performance was not an isolated flash of quality. His underlying numbers suggest he has been an important creative presence despite struggling to produce consistent end product.

According to FotMob, he has recorded three assists and created 30 chances in 16 Championship appearances this season, including seven big chances. His expected assists figure of 2.72 also suggests he has been unfortunate not to receive greater reward for his creativity.

He has additionally completed 21 dribbles, won 66 duels and drawn 26 fouls, demonstrating his ability to carry the ball, beat opponents and make things happen in the final third. However, there is still room for improvement.

Mane is yet to score in the Championship this season, despite registering 34 shots and accumulating an expected goals figure of 2.17. His average FotMob rating of 6.95 reflects a campaign containing some encouraging contributions but also inconsistent performances.

Mane has previously admitted that he has not been at his best, explaining after the Charlton victory that he needed a performance like this to get assists into his system. That honesty is encouraging, particularly given his age and the expectations surrounding him.

He has already shown promise at Premier League level, scoring against West Ham in January 2026, and Wolves clearly believe there is considerably more to come. The teenager had plenty of interest from Premier League sides in the summer but remained at Molineux.

Perhaps the most encouraging aspect of Mane’s afternoon was the public backing he received from Peixoto. The Wolves head coach acknowledged the teenager’s talent while recognising that consistency can be difficult for young players.

He insisted the coaching staff would not give up on him and emphasised the importance of allowing him to play with confidence and courage without placing excessive pressure on his shoulders. That is precisely the environment Mane needs.

Wolves are firmly involved in the promotion picture, and Mane could become increasingly important as the season progresses. His performance against Charlton offered a glimpse of his considerable potential, but the next challenge is turning those moments into regular contributions.

If he can add goals to his growing creative output, Wolves could have an outstanding attacking talent capable of making a significant difference to their promotion ambitions.

#2042. What happened weeks before Ian Holloway's Swindon Town sacking is revealing

Ian Holloway has been dismissed as Swindon Town boss following their 2-0 defeat at Newport County, but the search for a new manager has been ongoing at the County Ground for several weeks now.

Holloway was one of the managers who came into the new season under a little bit of pressure, mainly due to how last season ended. The Robins were sitting second in mid-February but fell out of the play-offs completely after four wins in their final 15 games.

The former Blackpool and Crystal Palace boss still had some credit in the bank after guiding Swindon from relegation danger in his first half-season at the club, but after picking up just 19 points from 16 games to start this term, the higher-ups at the County Ground have pulled the trigger.

This hasn't been a rash decision, though, and apparently one that they've been preparing for for around a month.

According to the Moonraker, Swindon's search for a new manager to replace Ian Holloway has been ongoing "in some form or another" for over a month.

The Robins endured a mixed start to the 2026/27 campaign. They won each of their opening three games at the County Ground, but paired that with terrible away form, picking up just one point from their first five on the road.

Before that 1-0 defeat at Rochdale, which made it four losses from five away, Holloway labelled himself a "dead man walking" if he couldn't help turn their form around amidst a terrible injury crisis. It turns out, from this report, that he was starting to become one.

In fact, a shock 3-1 win at league leaders Barnet just over a fortnight later, in turn handing them their first league defeat of the season and their first league loss since March, seemed to give him an extra bit of time, especially as that was followed up by a draw at Bristol Rovers and a win over Rotherham United on Halloween.

It's been four straight defeats in all competitions since then, though, albeit two away against League One sides MK Dons and Plymouth Argyle in the FA Cup and Jersey Mike's Trophy, respectively.

That's been enough to earn him the sack, and that work which has been done in the background will now need to be the full focus for the higher-ups at Swindon.

It's probably not an unpopular belief that Holloway may have been dismissed after that Barnet game if the result went the way many expected it to go. Not only were Swindon winless in their first five on the road, but they hadn't scored, either.

In the end, while the win against the high-flying Bees was impressive, it was done by unsustainable means. The Robins were 3-0 up at half-time, having scored all their shots on target. Meanwhile, Dean Brennan's side created 2.54xG in that first half, with Conor Ripley having to make eight saves before the break. He ended the game with 11.

Ranking the Championship clubs based on how successful they've been since the start of 2001/02

A win against a hapless Rotherham side isn't exactly convincing, either, and, ultimately, their other wins outside of that Barnet game haven't aged that well at all.

Cheltenham Town aside, they prevailed against Port Vale in September, who took a while to shake off their relegation hangover, and Colchester United, who are among the bottom four. They've also not scored away from home in the league since that Barnet game.

In the end, it seemed as if Swindon wanted Holloway to turn things around, but had seen enough after another defeat to nil on the road at Newport.

An exchange with a fan after the game that the former Robins boss needed to be restrained by stewards for meant that the writing was on the wall.

Who do you think Swindon Town will turn to after dismissing Ian Holloway? Let us know in the comments.

League Two has been so competitive so far this season, though, that Swindon are only four points off a play-off spot and six off the final automatic promotion place, too, despite just two wins in their last 11 league outings.

The right appointment here could not only save their season from the possibility of relegation, but it could also push them towards League One. Only time will tell who Swindon turn to next, but it seems like they've been preparing for who that may be for a little while now.

#2040. West Ham watched £3.3m Cardiff City star – fresh January move makes sense

Cardiff City have made an encouraging start under the excellent Brian Barry-Murphy, while West Ham United's stature, financial muscle, and expectations mean anything other than a serious promotion challenge would represent disappointment.

However, Scott Parker still has plenty of work to do in shaping his squad and implementing his ideas. The January transfer window could present an opportunity to address one particular weakness, with Yousef Salech an increasingly attractive option after previously attracting West Ham's attention.

West Ham have scored plenty of goals this season, so finding the back of the net has not necessarily been their biggest problem. However, neither Nuno Espirito Santo nor Scott Parker has managed to identify a reliable focal point to spearhead the attack.

Joel Piroe, Taty Castellanos, and Pablo have all flattered to deceive in that role. Each possesses some useful attributes, but none has consistently demonstrated the ability to lead the line and provide the presence required to bring others into play.

The 25 top scorers in the Championship since the second tier was rebranded back in 2004/05, including Billy Sharp and Aleksandar Mitrovic.

That is where Cardiff could come into the picture. West Ham have previously been credited with interest in Cardiff striker Salech, and they should seriously consider firming that up in January.

His development has been particularly encouraging, having kicked on well this season. The £3.3 million-rated Cardiff forward could give Parker the focal point his new-look side is missing. The 24-year-old would almost certainly cost much more than that currently, though.

Salech offers considerably more than just a physical presence through the middle, with his ability to occupy defenders complemented by an increasingly impressive goalscoring record.

He has nine goals in all competitions this season, including six in the Championship after his strike against Norwich City over the weekend, but the Dane's underlying numbers are arguably even more encouraging from the perspective of any potential suitors.

Yousef Salech's Cardiff City seasons (all comps)

Despite playing for a newly-promoted side, he boasts the second-highest xG in the division at 8.6, according to FotMob, behind only Middlesbrough's Will Lankshear. Cardiff teammate Jack Moylan is third with 7.7, highlighting the excellent positions Barry-Murphy is getting his attacking players into.

Given he has six goals from that, Salech has been profligate with some of those opportunities, but his consistent ability to find time and space inside the box is testament to both his movement and the coaching he is receiving with Cardiff.

There is an argument that West Ham have more pressing concerns than another striker, given how unbalanced their summer recruitment has left the squad. Naturally, after relegation, there was a decent overhaul of the squad.

They have plenty of quality, but only one attack-minded right-back in Kyle Walker-Peters, no reliable left-back thus far, and no defensive midfielder has established themselves as the ideal partner for Arne Engels at the base of midfield for a team chasing automatic promotion from the Championship.

What would be your priority for West Ham in January? Comment below!

Playing central defenders at full-back is hardly conducive to a side that should be dominating opponents with two overlapping players providing width. Their goalkeepers have also underwhelmed, despite Mads Hermansen previously demonstrating his quality at this level with Leicester City.

Addressing those weaknesses arguably takes priority, but that should not prevent Parker from pursuing Salech if the opportunity arises. A genuine focal point could make the difference in turning West Ham's attacking quality into a promotion-winning side. Cardiff's asking price may be beyond them, though.

#2039. £2.5m man’s performance against Swansea City could be the breakout West Brom have been waiting for: Opinion

West Brom extended their unbeaten Championship run to seven games on Saturday with a 2-2 draw away at Swansea City. WBA took the lead twice but were pegged back on both occasions, with their biggest frustration being a missed opportunity to move 3-1 ahead.

Kareem Tunde took a significant step towards establishing himself at West Brom with an impressive display in draw. The winger provided the assist for Isaac Price’s early opener, offering a glimpse of the quality that convinced Albion to bring him to The Hawthorns from Levante in the summer.

It was only Tunde’s second Championship start of the season, having previously been restricted to brief substitute appearances. However, James Morrison’s decision to reward his impressive cameo against Millwall proved inspired.

Given space on the right in the seventh minute, the 21-year-old produced a low, sweeping delivery across the box, allowing Price to arrive at the far post and finish. Tunde continued to cause problems before being substituted after 75 minutes, having reportedly struggled with cramp.

Tunde’s performance was reflected in a 7.7 FotMob rating, his highest of the campaign. However, even without that assist, the winger showed he had plenty to offer and a lot more to his game.

He also registered one assist, created a big chance and recorded an impressive 0.81 expected assists (xA), according to FotMob. Those numbers underline his creative influence, particularly considering he has played just 195 Championship minutes across eight appearances this season.

There is plenty of potential for West Brom to develop. Tunde has managed nine shots, created four chances and completed three crosses in the league, while his 11 line-breaking passes demonstrate an ability to move possession into dangerous areas.

His successful dribble percentage remains low at 20 per cent, and he is still learning when to make the right decisions in the final third. Nevertheless, those imperfections should not overshadow the encouraging signs.

Tunde arrived with an interesting pedigree, having spent time in Barcelona’s famed La Masia academy between 2017 and 2020 before progressing through Levante’s system. He also gained valuable La Liga experience, making more than 20 senior appearances before joining WBA on a four-year contract in August.

The Express & Star described him as raw but lively, highlighting his unpredictability, clever body movement and potential to trouble defenders. His combination play with right-back Brayann Pereira was another positive from the Swansea encounter.

West Brom ultimately dropped two points despite taking the lead twice, but Tunde’s contribution was an encouraging individual development. With the Baggies having several injury concerns at the moment, and competition for attacking places remaining fierce, the youngster has an opportunity to build on this performance.

Albion have been waiting to see what their summer investment can offer, and Saturday’s outing may finally have provided the breakthrough.

#2036. Jürgen Klopp has an unexpected connection to West Ham’s January plans

Despite a change in the dugout, West Ham’s woes in the Championship have continued.

Scott Parker is yet to taste victory since taking over from Nuno Espírito Santo, with a 1-1 draw at home to bottom of the table Preston North End last weekend typifying their early-season struggles.

The Hammers now sit 10th in the second tier standings after 16 league games, with it difficult to see when and how the East London outfit are going to get back on an upward trajectory.

One way could be to address certain positions in the January transfer window, but yet again, there are so many games to come in a congested winter period, so it’s important that from somewhere, West Ham find a bit of magic to keep themselves in the promotion mix.

A position West Ham have really struggled with so far this season is the lack of a clinical frontman, with none of Taty Castellanos, Joel Piroe or Pablo contributing enough on a consistent basis.

Meanwhile, one forward they let go of in the summer, Niclas Füllkrug, is enjoying such a fruitful spell back in Germany on loan at Werder Bremen, so much so he’s earned recognition from national team manager Jürgen Klopp.

Such a rise back to form may give the Hammers an interesting decision to make in January as to whether they get the forward back in the fold or sell him for a sizeable fee at the turn of the new year.

Niclas Füllkrug arrived at West Ham with quite the reputation.

After netting 15 and assisting 10 in all competitions for Borussia Dortmund in the 2023-24 campaign, he followed that up with a couple of goals for Germany in Euro 2024, peaking the Hammers’ interest.

West Ham thought they were bringing such goalscoring prowess to England following the tournament, spending £27 million to sign the then-31-year-old, but his time at the London Stadium was nothing short of disastrous.

Füllkrug’s first season at the club was marred by injuries, with the German starting just six times and scoring three goals in the process.

West Ham are determined to keep Konstantinos Mavropanos even if there is interest in the centre-back in the January window.

Last year, he made just eight league appearances before being deemed surplus to requirements and loaned out to AC Milan, where he scored just once in under 650 minutes of football.

It wasn't exactly a spell which warranted a return to the West Ham fold ahead of a Championship return, and the 33-year-old was loaned back to Werder Bremen during the summer, with it expected that he'd see out the final two years of his Hammers contract elsewhere.

Many connected with West Ham will be amazed at the turnaround Niclas Füllkrug has made on his return to Germany, demonstrating himself to be one of the most lethal finishers in the Bundesliga this season.

Füllkrug has found his form back in familar surroundings, with his last campaign with Bremen in 2022-23 resulting in a 16-goal return,

But the 33-year-old is well on his way to surpassing that number, netting 10 goals in as many appearances so far in the German top-flight.

West Ham fans, can you quite believe Niclas Füllkrug's revival at Werder Bremen? Comment your thoughts below!

Füllkrug is currently the top scorer in the division, one ahead of Bayer Leverkusen’s Patrik Schick and six ahead of Ballon D’Or winner Harry Kane in an unprecedented revival.

With Niclas Füllkrug banging in goals for fun and his confidence sky-high, there was no surprise to see him raise eyebrows on the national front.

And at the start of November, a report by German outlet Kicker revealed that the Werder Bremen frontman had been recalled to the national team by Jürgen Klopp for their Nations League fixtures against Serbia and the Netherlands.

Klopp was very complimentary of Füllkrug’s form too, saying: "Good form and scoring goals are never a bad thing.

"Fülle has been performing and scoring all kinds of goals, almost all of them spectacular. He has an incredible influence on Werder Bremen, not just through his goals, but also through the way he carries himself."

Such impressive words from Klopp are testament to the impact Füllkrug has had on his return to Germany, and it leaves West Ham with a big decision to make as the January transfer window ticks down.

Füllkrug remains contracted to the Hammers as he’s only on a season-long loan at Bremen, but Werder’s managing director, Clemens Fritz, has publicly expressed interest in signing the striker permanently, although he stressed that any agreement must involve all three parties of Bremen, West Ham and the player.

The West Ham faithful will be bemoaning the fact Niclas Füllkrug failed to show much goalscoring ability at the London Stadium, but then returning to Germany has him placed as one of the league’s top performing players.

And while their association with the German frontman on the pitch has been a complete waste of time, there could be some light at the end of the tunnel as to how much money they can get for him.

Despite having just two years left on his contract, Werder Bremen clearly value Füllkrug highly given the impact he’s made on his return, and with their managing director already showing his interest in making the Weserstadion Füllkrug's home again on a permanent basis, it’s evidence enough that the German outfit would be willing to pay quite a lot of money to keep hold of the Bundesliga’s top scorer.

Werder Bremen are believed to not have an option to buy in the original loan deal, so the ball is very much in West Ham’s court when it comes to negotiating terms.

The Hammers must stand firm when it comes to a price, with the club having to hold out to get as much back on the £27m deal they signed off to bring him to the London Stadium in the first place.

Praying on Werder Bremen’s desperation, such a lump of cash could have a significant say on their January transfer plans, with much of that money being used to bring in a new striker that could help turn the Hammers’ fortunes around and still have them in contention for a place back in the Premier League.

So while much of Füllkrug's stint at West Ham has been filled with bitter disappointment, it could end up having the perfect ending and playing a huge part in Scott Parker’s men achieving their promotion dreams in May.

Manchester United have been linked with Como midfielder Nico Paz, although Real Madrid’s buy-back option and the absence of reported negotiations leave considerable uncertainty over a potential move.

The 22-year-old Argentina international has impressed in Italy this season, scoring six goals and providing eight assists in 14 appearances. According to The Sun, United have been monitoring Paz ahead of a possible summer transfer, with Mauricio Pochettino’s appointment potentially helping their chances.

Paz joined Como from Real Madrid in 2024 and has become an important player under Cesc Fabregas. He scored twice and provided an assist in Como’s 4-1 Champions League victory over United last month.

Transfer journalist Fabrizio Romano has questioned the strength of United’s reported interest, saying there has been no contact or serious negotiation. Romano explained that the link had emerged because the same agency represents Paz and Pochettino.

He also suggested that Madrid remain central to the player’s future and that a January move is not being considered. United could assess their attacking midfield options next summer, with captain Bruno Fernandes’ contract situation still unresolved.

For now, Paz remains a reported target rather than a player involved in active transfer talks.

#2032. Who stays and who leaves Manchester United under Mauricio Pochettino, Gary Neville reveals list

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United hired Mauricio Pochettino as their next manager to replace Michael Carrick, who managed just one win in 10 games.

His appointment makes him Manchester United’s eighth permanent manager since Sir Alex Ferguson retired in 2013.

Pochettino takes charge of Manchester United sitting in an unfamiliar 18th in the Premier League table with only one win from 10 matches. He has a massive job ahead of him, with many pundits and former players terming his situation the toughest in the Premier League.

Gary Neville believes the Argentine will need several transfer windows to get the squad where he wants it, if he gets that much of time at Manchester United.

The former captain shared his views on which players he expects Pochettino to keep, and who could be heading for the exit.

Neville believes Senne Lammens should remain part of the plans, although he expects Pochettino to bring in another goalkeeper to give him some competition for his place. At the back, he backed Luke Shaw, Harry Maguire and Leny Yoro to stay, while suggesting Ayden Heaven needs a loan move to the Championship.

“Heaven, I’d loan him out, get some experience in the Championship. He’s nowhere near it, it’s unfair to put him in the situation he’s in,” Neville said on Stick to Football.

In midfield, Neville wants Pochettino to build around Bruno Fernandes, Kobbie Mainoo and Youri Tielemans. He also believes Andrey Santos could have a future at the club because of his age, mobility and aggression.

Mason Mount, however, is not part of his plans. Neville said: “Mount would have to go” and identified him as the player to leave from that midfield group.

Neville also has his doubts on the forward line, with much of the front line seeing a change. Gary Neville expects Joshua Zirkzee to leave and believes United cannot continue paying Marcus Rashford’s wages.

“Zirkzee definitely to go. I think Marcus 100 per cent to go because he’s on massive money and that story has been well told. They can’t afford to pay him that money, you have to get rid of that wage at the earliest opportunity.”

He expects Pochettino to work with Amad Diallo, Bryan Mbeumo, Matheus Cunha and Benjamin Sesko. Patrick Dorgu could also be useful in the short term, although Neville thinks he may leave within the next couple of years. Manuel Ugarte, meanwhile, was another player he was certain would go.

Talking about the exits, Gary Neville has singled out many United stars to be shown the door in the first wave of squad clearance by Mauricio Pochettino. Neville identified Lisandro Martínez, Matthijs de Ligt and Noussair Mazraoui as further candidates to leave in the first wave of departures, alongside Mount, Rashford and Zirkzee.

Neville believes another group of exits could follow a year later, with Shaw, Maguire, Diogo Dalot and Fernandes among the players whose futures may not be at the club in long term. Pochettino would also have to decide whether Santos and Carlos Baleba fit into his long-term plans.

Neville does not expect United to rebuild the squad in a single summer, even if Pochettino makes a positive start.

“But the earliest Mauricio Pochettino can unravel this squad is two seasons if he does well. It could take three to get this squad unravelled.”

Pochettino’s first game comes against Liverpool at Anfield on Sunday. His immediate priority will be to get some results for Manchester United but beyond the immediate games and results, Manchester United are expected to make multiple exits from the club as Pochettino settles into the job.

#2031. Mauricio Pochettino set to get involved in Bruno Fernandes contract talks

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Fernandes has been one of United’s most influential players since arriving from Sporting CP in January 2020.

Bruno Fernandes has scored 113 goals in 341 appearances for Manchester United and has regularly carried the creative responsibility in midfield for the Red Devils.

His performances have remained amazing despite United struggling in recent times. Despite a sluggish start to the last season, Bruno Fernandes went on to register 21 Premier League assists, breaking the league’s single-season record.

Mauricio Pochettino will now have the opportunity to speak directly with the midfielder and make his own case for him to remain part of the project.

However, there are several issues still to resolve. The financial terms, length of the proposed contract and release clause will all need to be discussed before an agreement can be reached with the Portuguese midfielder.

Mauricio Pochettino is preparing to hold talks with Bruno Fernandes over his Manchester United future, with the club captain yet to agree a new contract at Old Trafford.

According to Fabrizio Romano, the new United head coach will now become involved in discussions with the Portuguese midfielder, whose current deal expires at the end of the season. The club have the option to extend it by a further 12 months, but are also keen to secure a longer-term agreement.

Manchester United have already held negotiations with Fernandes, but the 32-year-old has rejected two contract offers. Romano explained that Pochettino’s involvement could be an important part of the talks, although the final decision will rest with Bruno Fernandes and United.

Speaking on his YouTube channel, Fabrizio Romano said: “First point: Mauricio Pochettino will be involved now in discussions with Bruno Fernandes.

“So, Pochettino will talk to Bruno, and he’s going to enter into this conversation.

“Then it’s going to be on Bruno, it’s going to be on the club.

“It’s a financial point, length of the contract, the release clause.

“It’s not Pochettino deciding yes or not, but Pochettino will be another factor now in the conversation.

Fernandes’ future is not settled at Manchester United and with Pochettino arriving at the club, he may have a different thought about his contract situation. Although a deal is not finalized yet but the talks will give the new head coach a chance to understand Fernandes’ position. However, the final call remains with Bruno Fernandes.

#2030. Andre's Wolves future has a 2031 twist amid Cesar Peixoto's January warning

Wolves are starting to build momentum as they target an immediate return to the Premier League under Cesar Peixoto.

The Black Country outfit were always expected to be in the mix for promotion due to the quality of the squad they boast after eight years in the Premier League.

Whilst there was a feeling that Wolves could’ve spent more in the summer, they did manage to retain several key players, and the likes of Ladislav Krejčí and Andre are emerging as huge figures for Peixoto.

The latter has started every Championship game this season, which speaks volumes to his importance when you consider how often Peixoto has rotated.

Yet, he recognises the qualities that Andre brings, and the reality is that even though Wolves have some good midfield options on the bench, the Brazilian is easily the strongest defensive midfielder in the squad.

Therefore, it will come as no surprise that Peixoto has already warned Wolves against selling the former Fluminense man in the New Year, as he told the club’s media that a sale must not be on the agenda.

“I don’t need to say how important he is for us. We have to keep him with us until the end of the season. He’s a very, very important player, a key player for us.

“I hope, and I expect, that he stays with us. In my head, I don’t want to hear anything about Andre leaving. It is impossible to be clearer than that. For sure, there’ll be interest in him in January as he’s a great player, but he’s our player, and he’s going to stay with us.”

Following on from that, you could make a strong case that Andre is the most important player in the Wolves squad right now.

Such is the strength in depth at Molineux, Wolves have two players for every position, and they’ve coped reasonably well without Raul Jimenez for a chunk of the campaign already.

Boubacar Traore is a decent backup to Andre, but he doesn’t possess the same attributes as the 25-year-old, and his fitness is a concern as well.

The Old Gold and Black have put themselves in a strong position to achieve a first-time promotion

At his best, Andre is the man who makes things tick for Wolves. He plays with real intensity in shutting down the opposition, and his tactical understanding of the game ensures he is always in the right place. Then, on the ball, he is effective at moving it quickly, and he is capable of switching play.

Andre also relishes the physical side of the Championship, and his presence in the middle of the park is a major factor in why Wolves have the best xG against in the division.

Peixoto’s comments about Andre were understandable, as there will be interest in the midfielder, but the pleasing thing for Wolves is that they hold all the cards when it comes to negotiations.

That’s because, to the shock of many, Andre signed a new long-term contract at Wolves shortly after the Premier League season finished.

Clearly, he has bought into what the club is about, and he was obviously sold on the long-term project at Molineux.

The new contract was an initial four-year agreement, with Wolves having an option to extend it by another 12 months.

Of course, that doesn’t mean Andre can’t be sold, but many would’ve had the 13-cap Brazil international to be one of the certainties to leave after relegation.

So, the fact he didn’t push for a move back then, and actually committed his future to the club, suggests he is happy to stick around for at least one year.

With Wolves in a position to push for the top two, it’s unlikely that Andre’s situation will have changed too much at the moment, and his focus will be on helping the club back to the Premier League.

Looking beyond that, it seems inevitable that Andre will leave Wolves if they don’t win promotion this season.

Obviously, he won’t want another year in the Championship, and the club would be in a different financial position if they don’t go up, and Andre is undoubtedly a prized asset.

Will Wolves win promotion this season? Let us know in the comments below.

But, that is a discussion for the summer, and all connected to Wolves will hope that Peixoto’s demands are met, which means Andre will see the season out.

If he does, and he maintains the levels he has set in the opening months, then there’s every chance Wolves will be celebrating promotion come May, and the midfielder will have made himself a hero at Molineux after showing remarkable loyalty following relegation.

#2029. How can Mauricio Pochettino help Manchester United players to perform again?

Mauricio Pochettino has called on Manchester United to rediscover their winning mentality after agreeing to become the club’s new head coach.

Manchester United announced Pochettino’s appointment just over 24 hours after confirming the departure of Michael Carrick. The former Chelsea and Tottenham manager is believed to have agreed a two-and-a-half-year deal at Old Trafford.

He takes charge of United who sit in an embarrassing 18th place in the Premier League table. They have managed just one win in their opening 10 league matches, coming against Ipswich Town.

There is little time for the new head coach to settle in, with a difficult first fixture against fierce rivals, Liverpool already awaiting him.

Speaking to Manchester United, Mauricio Pochettino made it clear that restoring confidence in the team and improving performances will be his immediate priorities.

“The most important thing for now is to start to work hard to recover that feeling about being a winner – to win games, to perform.”

The Argentine hinted at sticking with the current crop of players to help them rediscover their form.

“We need to love the players that we have. We need to support the players, we need to provide all the tools for them to perform.”

The new head coach faces a mountain ahead of him. He has walked into one of the most difficult jobs in world football. United have struggled so far into the season and they need results quickly to get them out of the relegation spots.

Pochettino has spoken about bringing back the winning feeling at Old Trafford, but his first challenge will be getting a response from his players who have managed only one league win in 10 games so far. That is far from Manchester United standards and Pochettino will be hoping he can instill some confidence back in the team.

The Liverpool game will offer an early indication of how his players respond to the change at United. A positive result would provide immediate relief for the under pressure United but the longer-term job of rebuilding United will take much more than one match.

#2028. What did the secret drone footage reveal about Manchester United training?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Mauricio Pochettino’s first Manchester United training session was secretly filmed by a drone operator, who has since apologised and removed the footage from his YouTube channel after admitting his actions were unethical.

The footage showed players split into smaller groups carrying out different drills. On a separate full-size pitch, 11 players were positioned across the field alongside mannequins before gathering to receive instructions and feedback from new head coach Mauricio Pochettino.

Ten players were seen working with Pochettino in the video but only Harry Maguire, Kobbie Mainoo, Amad Diallo, Matheus Cunha and Bryan Mbeumo are regular starters among those.

Another group involved in drills was that of Lisandro Martinez, Matthijs de Ligt, Youri Tielemans, Mason Mount, Leny Yoro, Noussair Mazraoui and Diogo Dalot.

In his statement, the operator said the flight itself was legal under drone regulations but accepted that recording the training session was wrong.

He explained that his intention was to give Manchester United supporters their first look at Pochettino wearing the club’s training kit.

He said: “A total error of judgement on my part led to me filming some of the Manchester United training session at Carrington, whilst the flight was legal in terms of drone laws I fully accept it was unethical on my part.”

The operator apologized directly to the club, its new head coach and the players.

“I would like to apologise to Manchester United, Mauricio Pochettino and the players for my actions and sincerely hope those actions do not affect the outcome of any match.”

He also rejected any suggestion that Liverpool or another football club had been involved in his decision to film at Carrington.

“The video has been removed from my channel,” he added.

#2027. Jamie Carragher questions Mauricio Pochettino’s Manchester United appointment

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher believes Mauricio Pochettino has as much to prove as Manchester United, suggesting the Argentine’s reputation is not fully backed up by his achievements.

Mauricio Pochettino is preparing for his first match in charge of Manchester United against Liverpool at Anfield on Sunday, following his departure from the United States national team to replace Michael Carrick.

The former Tottenham and Chelsea manager has arrived at Old Trafford with plenty of experience, but Carragher believes his appointment represents an opportunity for both United and Pochettino to rebuild their reputations.

Writing in The Telegraph, the former Liverpool defender highlighted the irony of United turning to a manager whose own career needs a fresh start.

“It is ironic that in their latest attempt to rejuvenate and restore some of their old fire, Manchester United have turned to a manager whose career needs a similar reawakening,” Carragher wrote.

He suggested that Pochettino should be grateful that a club of United’s stature came calling at this stage of his career.

“Manchester United need a savior to first ease them from the relegation zone and then revive their Premier League and Champions League credibility. Pochettino, meanwhile, needed a club like United to save him from a managerial career that promised more than it delivered.”

Manchester United were fortunate to secure a manager with Pochettino’s experience after dismissing Michael Carrick so early in the season. However, he believes the Argentine has plenty to prove despite his strong reputation in the game.

“The South American returns to England with a big reputation, but not enough on his CV to back up glowing testimonials,” he continued.

Carrick believes one of the biggest concerns surrounding Pochettino is his record in important matches.

“A recurring issue with Pochettino, which continued with the US team, is an inability to get over the line in the most consequential, winnable matches, resulting in a lack of big trophies.”

Despite questioning his ability to bottle in big games, he praised his teams when everything comes in together.

“Ten years ago his best team were the embodiment of him, full of energy, style and ambition. Time will tell whether this version of Pochettino is the one turning up at Old Trafford.”

Pochettino will have the chance to shut Carragher’s doubts on Sunday when Manchester United face Liverpool. His immediate priority will be to take United out of the relegation zone, but the expectations at Old Trafford will extend far beyond survival.

The Liverpool fixture will be his first opportunity to make an impression. Carragher, however, believes the Argentine’s real challenge is to turn his reputation into success.

#2026. Top 7 best value-for-money EFL League One signings this season

League One clubs are always looking for value-for-money signings.

The majority of clubs in the third tier don't have millions and millions to throw around, so smart additions that can either be flipped for big profits in the future, or even those you bring in to perform one role and who can perform it perfectly, go a long way toward achieving success in the division.

This list won't just run down the best signings in League One so far this season, but instead look at those who have made such a massive impact that you can't help but feel the selling side should be given a little more money in retrospect.

Here are the seven best 'value for money' signings in League One this season.

A 32-year-old Jayden Stockley moving from Port Vale after their relegation to AFC Wimbledon isn't going to grab headlines at the time, but for a Dons side who know their backs are against the wall and will be forced to play direct, the frontman has been perfect for them.

Stockley reunited with ex-Charlton Athletic boss Johnnie Jackson in the summer, and was handed one role: win aerial duels and get us up the field. Now 33, the target man has been brilliant.

He's been involved in 220 aerial duels, 81 more than anyone else in the division. The 112 he's won is 27 more than Paudie O'Connor in second place.

Plus, he's scored six league goals, which amounts to 60% of his side's total output. He's one of the main reasons why AFC Wimbledon are currently above the drop zone, and if they stay there, he'll be a key part of why, too.

Wigan Athletic had a big task on their hands this past summer in replacing academy graduate Sam Tickle after the goalkeeper made his well-deserved step-up to the Championship with Bristol City.

It seemed inevitable that the man to do so would be Joe Whitworth, whom Tics boss Gary Caldwell had loaned twice while at Exeter City from Crystal Palace, and they did so in a rather interesting deal.

The 22-year-old joined on a free transfer, with Palace holding a sell-on fee, meaning that Wigan will only have to pay the Premier League side a share of a future deal, rather than part ways with money now.

So far this season, Whitworth has prevented the most goals in the division, saved shots at a 70.2% rate and kept three clean sheets in 12 games, while also having above-average distribution numbers.

He's quickly establishing himself as one of the best in his position, and for a free transfer, that's not bad at all.

It'll be mentioned a little later on in this list, but the ability to bring in someone who had such a great loan spell the season prior on a permanent deal for a cut-price, or in this case, for free, is incredible business.

Jon Russell has been revived under Nigel Clough at Mansfield Town after a strangely poor half-year at Barnsley under Conor Hourihane, after he netted 11 in 2024/25. He spent the latter half of last year at Field Mill, scoring five and assisting one in 21 league games.

The 26-year-old made his move permanent for free and has five goals and three assists in 13 games in all competitions so far this year for the Stags. He's been one of the best goalscoring midfielders in the division, and Clough seems to be playing to his strengths.

Mansfield continue to upset the odds and break new ceilings, and it's deals like this which help them do so.

Bradford City were among the best defensive sides in League One last season. Only four teams conceded fewer goals. They arguably didn't need to replace Sam Walker in goal, but if the right deal was there, Bantams fans were open to the idea.

In came Jon McCracken from Dundee FC on a free transfer, who had recorded the most saves and prevented 7.53 goals in the SPL last season. He was even linked with Celtic back in April. So, getting him for nothing was already a good start.

Ranking the Championship clubs based on how successful they've been since the start of 2001/02

In his first 17 games as a Bantam, the Scotsman has registered 12 clean sheets, including nine in 12 league outings. Before shipping three against Huddersfield Town, he'd conceded just four goals in 11 games.

That's quite exceptional, and the only thing perhaps limiting the amount of praise he should rightly get is the fact that Bradford as a whole are so great defensively. McCracken has only had to make 19 league saves all season, but he's still pulling through when called into action.

Derensili Sanches Fernandes has been the best player in League One this season. The man coined the 'dribble king' in the Dutch top flight at Excelsior has, unsurprisingly, been electric going forward against third-tier opposition, and has 13 goal involvements in 14 games.

Those involvements are as evenly split as you can imagine, too, with seven goals and six assists. Not only is he a great finisher, but he sets up chances better than anyone else, too. Indeed, no player has created more chances, nor big chances, than the 25-year-old.

He's even been capped by Cape Verde for the first time due to the quality he has shown at the Accu Stadium, and scored on his debut against Liberia in AFCON qualifiers.

But as good as Sanches Fernandes has been, he wasn't exactly cheap. Reports state that he moved to Huddersfield from Excelsior for "a significant" undisclosed fee, which could indicate a seven-figure sum. From that point of view, there are better value-for-money signings this season.

For the impact the wideman has made at his new club, though, Town fans feel like they may want to send the Dutch side a little more.

Luton Town's upturn in form towards the end of the season last year was not only down to Jack Wilshere becoming more settled in the dugout, but also due to the brilliance of Kasey Palmer, who joined on loan from Hull City.

In their 11-game unbeaten run to end the campaign, Palmer netted eight and assisted once. He also started in their EFL Trophy final win at Wembley against Stockport County. The Jamaican international had never played below the Championship before in his career, and was showcasing why.

It was imperative that Palmer made a permanent move if Luton were to continue their momentum from the latter stages of the previous year and go at least one place better than their seventh-place finish, and they managed to secure his services for less than £500,000.

For an attacking midfielder in his prime years, whose level is above League One, that's a brilliant bit of business, and he already has three goals and four assists in 12 league starts this season.

If he could help Jack Wilshere's side get back into the second-tier, it would be money well spent.

In terms of signing someone with big potential to be worth a lot more than the six-figure sum they initially paid, and who could help their side in the meantime push for promotion, no one comes close to Kyreece Lisbie.

Reports over the summer before a deal was made between Reading and Colchester United suggested that a fee between £500,000 and £750,000 would be needed, as Lisbie entered the final year of his deal at the League Two side, and already, he has the potential to be worth a decent seven-figure sum come the end of the campaign.

Who do you think has been the best value for money signing in League One so far this season? Let us know in the comments.

The pacy wideman has five goals and five assists in 15 games so far this season for Leam Richardson's side, profiting from the prolific nature of Jack Marriott but also getting into good goalscoring positions himself to help the Royals stay in and around the top six.

His twin brother, Kyrell, was sold to Millwall for between £2.5m and £3m this past summer after scoring 11 and assisting seven for Peterborough United. Kyreece is already over halfway to that goal-contribution tally and is performing for a side higher up in the division.

If he can keep this up, not only could he help Reading reach the second tier, but he could also bank the Royals a healthy profit immediately. Evidently, he has been the best value-for-money signing in League One so far.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Bruno Fernandes has been at the center of contract talks this season and is yet to sign a new deal at Manchester United.

Fernandes’ contract expires at the end of the season, leaving Manchester United with an important decision to make over the future of their captain.

#2023. Liverpool vs Man Utd: Preview, key stats, team news & where to watch

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Mauricio Pochettino begins his Manchester United reign with a huge test at Anfield on Sunday, with Manchester United desperate for a result after making their worst start to a top-flight season in 40 years.

Manchester United have collected just eight points from their opening 10 Premier League matches, with one win and five draws leaving them 18th in the table. They have also conceded in every league game this season and are currently on a run of 11 consecutive matches without keeping a clean sheet in all competitions.

Mauricio Pochettino only arrived to the club on Wednesday after replacing Michael Carrick, leaving him with just a few training sessions to prepare for one of the biggest fixtures in English football.

There is some encouragement for the Argentine, however. Eight of United’s last nine permanent or caretaker managers have avoided defeat in their first match in charge. That may be some encouragement to the Argentine.

Meanwhile, Liverpool are not exactly enjoying a comfortable season at home either. They sit fourth with 18 points but have won just once in their five Premier League matches at Anfield so far, giving them the third-worst home record in the Premier League.

Their recent form across all competitions stands at three wins, one draw and two defeats in their last six matches. Liverpool most recently beat Crystal Palace 3-1, but they will know that United’s poor league position does not make this fixture any less complicated.

Liverpool have received positive news ahead of Sunday’s meeting. Dominik Szoboszlai and Virgil van Dijk are both expected to feature, while Ryan Gravenberch and Jeremie Frimpong could also be available.

Conor Bradley and Giovanni Leoni have returned to team training following knee injuries but are unlikely to make the squad. Hugo Ekitike remains a long-term absentee with an Achilles problem.

Manchester United could welcome back Patrick Dorgu and Harry Maguire after both trained following fitness concerns. They were seen in that leaked drone footage as well. Lisandro Martínez is also available again after serving his suspension.

Luke Shaw and Marcus Rashford could return, although their availability remains uncertain. Carlos Baleba, Manuel Ugarte and Benjamin Sesko are still sidelined.

The new United boss has a difficult personal record against Liverpool. Pochettino has faced the Reds 19 times, winning three, drawing five and losing 11. He has also failed to win any of his last eight meetings with them in all competitions. That might be a concern, but everything has a first time.

Pochettino will be encouraged by United’s overall record against Liverpool.  United have an edge with 85 wins compared to Liverpool’s 72 across 218 meetings. Their most recent encounter also went their way, with a 3-2 victory on May 3.

However, United arrive on a disappointing run of seven consecutive away matches without a win and have managed just one victory in their last 11 games.

#2022. Why can Mauricio Pochettino turn things around at Manchester United?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Mauricio Pochettino takes charge of his first United game against Liverpool at Anfield on Sunday, with plenty of questions surrounding the players who will benefit from his arrival.

Manchester United have struggled badly this season, sitting 18th in the Premier League after collecting just eight points from their opening 10 matches.

Pochettino replaces Michael Carrick and his job has been described as one of the toughest jobs in the Premier League. Former defender Wes Brown believes Manchester United have a chance with Pochettino to change things despite it being very difficult.

Brown believes the new manager has enough quality at his disposal to improve the situation, despite the difficult start and the pressure that comes with the Manchester United job.

Asked whether Pochettino faces an impossible task after previous managers failed to bring success to Old Trafford, Brown was confident the Argentine could make a difference.

“Definitely not impossible. Regardless of what has happened so far this season, Manchester United do have good players,” Wes Brown said while talking to Flashscore.

Brown expects Pochettino to demand more effort from the squad.

“He looks like a personable manager, so can work with these players individually to get their confidence up and really play for the shirt and create that positive vibe that seems to be missing.”

Asked about how long Manchester United will take to be a title challenger again, Wes Brown was cautious in putting any numbers.

“Well, it isn’t going to happen overnight. You will need a reaction quickly, that is for sure. They are very far away, but you have to start with consistency and that is something Pochettino has to install and deliver a consistency of play, to be fitter than your opponent and show fight.”

The former defender also highlighted Bruno Fernandes’ drop in form, suggesting the captain could benefit from a change in management.

“You have to see Bruno’s form has dropped, so it will be great to see this pick up under the new manager.”

Pochettino’s priority will be getting a response on Sunday against Liverpool from his senior players. United have been in this cycle of repeating managers and not finding much success. Mauricio Pochettino needs to take things one at a time. Sunday would be that first step.

#2021. Why Norwich City sit on a cash jackpot - it can make Spurs and Brentford jealous

Norwich City have won five games on the spin following their 2-1 home victory over Cardiff City at the weekend, and one player's upturn in form has coincided with this incredible streak.

Norwich's campaign thus far has been something of a rollercoaster, as, after 16 games, the Canaries are still yet to draw a game in the Championship this season, winning eight and losing eight.

Now on a five-game unbeaten run courtesy of victories over Charlton Athletic, Watford, Derby County, Bristol City, and Cardiff, Norwich have flown up the table. However, before this winning run came to fruition, a six-game losing streak across all competitions preceded it.

Belgian head coach Philippe Clement will certainly be hoping for more consistency moving forward, and with the January window approaching, may well also be looking to replicate some past transfers.

With Clement arriving at Carrow Road in November 2025, the former Monaco and Rangers boss has officially ticked over a year in charge in Norfolk, and it would be fair to suggest there have been more highs than lows during his reign.

The January 2026 window proved to be especially fruitful for the 52-year-old, who oversaw the additions of the likes of Sam Field, Ali Ahmed, Mohamed Toure, and Paris Maghoma at Norwich.

Maghoma, 25, joined from Premier League outfit Brentford in a deal worth around £800k, having worked his way up the EFL with loans at AFC Wimbledon, Milton Keynes Dons, and Bolton Wanderers, but failing to nail down a place in the Bees' side.

The former Tottenham Hotspur youth prospect scored once and grabbed three assists in 14 Championship games during his first six months at the club, but now has seemingly found his groove.

Football League World has asked AI to predict the 2027/28 EFL Championship table if Manchester United and Manchester City were in it

At the beginning of this campaign, Maghoma initially struggled to nail down a place in the Norwich starting XI, as after playing 71 minutes in the 2-1 home defeat to West Bromwich Albion in the season opener, the Englishman would have to wait nearly another month for his next league appearance.

He grabbed assists in defeats to Middlesbrough and Bolton, and after six defeats on the spin, Maghoma was finally introduced back into the starting lineup away to Charlton, grabbing the equaliser in a 3-1 victory.

Since then, the attacking midfielder has surpassed Anis Ben Slimane and Oscar Schwartau to a place in Clement's first XI, and with four goals in four games following his strike against Charlton, has duly rewarded the Belgian for his faith.

The attacking midfielder has found his stride in the Championship, and if he can continue this level of form, Norwich will certainly feel as though they have struck the transfer jackpot.

Having signed Maghoma for just £800k, Norwich will already be rubbing their hands at the prospect of a potential lucrative sale in the near future, especially if he can continue this rich vein of form.

The Englishman never managed to make a senior appearance during his time at Spurs, and with just ten first-team appearances for Brentford, his potential was never realised or displayed in West London.

Now, Norwich are reaping the rewards for taking the gamble and putting faith in Maghoma, as he could well help guide the Canaries back to the Premier League, or, at the very least, reward them with an enormous profit.

What have you made of Paris Maghoma's recent form for Norwich City? How much is he realistically worth now? Will Spurs and Brentford be feeling any transfer envy? Share your thoughts below!

With Toure and Ahmed out injured as of late, Clement has had to look elsewhere in his squad for somebody to take the mantle and become the main attacking threat in the side, and that appears to be Maghoma.

Mathias Kvistgaarden, Anthony Musaba, Andre Brooks, and Jovon Makama have each played their parts too, but it is the attacking midfielder who has drawn headlines for his performances and displays.

If Norwich are to achieve anything special this season, then Maghoma will surely be at the forefront of that success, as Spurs and Brentford may be feeling some transfer envy right now.

#2020. Manchester United managerial costs near £100m as Pochettino fee revealed

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Manchester United have spent approximately £95m appointing and dismissing managers since Sir Alex Ferguson retired in 2013, according to BBC Sport, with the total expected to pass £100m following Mauricio Pochettino’s arrival.

United’s latest financial disclosures confirm a fee of £7.584m in connection with appointing Pochettino, who left his role as United States national team head coach earlier this month.

The figure takes the cumulative cost of managerial changes to around £95m, according to BBC Sport’s analysis. That total is expected to rise once the costs of Michael Carrick’s dismissal and appointing Pochettino’s coaching staff are included.

Pochettino signed a two-and-a-half-year contract on 13 November, replacing Carrick after a difficult start to the Premier League season. The club’s Stock Market filing states that the appointment fee will be recognised in its financial accounts for the current quarter. United also confirmed borrowing a further £10m on 16 November, taking the amount drawn on their revolving credit facility to £240m.

Pochettino is United’s ninth full-time managerial appointment since Ferguson’s departure, including Ralf Rangnick’s interim spell. The club have also changed managers during each of the past three seasons.

United’s financial disclosures show that their long-term debt stood at £577.4m on 30 September, while outstanding transfer-fee commitments totalled £417m. Those figures are separate from the cost of appointing and dismissing managers.

A Reuters analysis has put the wider cost of managerial and senior football staff changes above £100m, using a broader calculation. Pochettino now faces the task of improving United’s results, beginning with Sunday’s Premier League trip to Liverpool.
