#2018. Bruno Fernandes breaks silence on Man Utd contract offer

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bruno Fernandes has been Manchester United’s most creative player since his arrival to the club in 2020.

The 32-year-old is approaching the final months of his current deal, which expires at the end of the season. United do have the option to extend his contract by another 12 months, keeping him at Old Trafford until 2028.

While they can extend his contract for a year, Manchester United are actively pursuing a deal to tie Bruno Fernandes beyond 2028.

Bruno Fernandes himself has now confirmed that the club have offered him a new contract, but the midfielder has made it clear that he does not want discussions about his future to become a priority right now.

Fernandes confirmed that an offer had been made but dismissed suggestions that he was holding out for a better salary or a longer agreement.

“The club offered me a new contract, so I think that, from their side, shows that they want me to stay,” he told Sky Sports.

He also insisted that money has never been the reason behind his decision to remain at United, despite having opportunities to move elsewhere in previous transfer windows.

“If I wanted more money, I wouldn’t be here,” Fernandes said.

The Portugal international added that he could have earned considerably more by accepting offers from other clubs, but always chose to continue his career in Manchester.

Fernandes revealed that discussions initially took place towards the end of last season and continued when the players returned for pre-season.

However, the United captain believes the current situation at the club makes it the wrong time to focus on his personal future.

“We came in at the beginning of this season speaking about the contract, but about the context of the team and the club, I just put that to a side,” he explained.

“I don’t think it’s the right moment, neither for me, nor for the club, to be talking about negotiations.”

Fernandes has been one of United’s most influential players since arriving from Sporting in 2020. He has scored 75 Premier League goals for the club and set a new competition record for assists in a single season last term.

Bruno’s comments don’t sound like he is holding United hostage demanding more pay or refusing to sign a new one. The intention as told by Bruno Fernandes just show his leadership trait about putting the team first. He has no immediate intention of making his contract situation the centre of attention. That will be a sign of encouragement for the fans as well.

Manchester United need to start winning games again. When they do and surely Bruno Fernandes will be at the centre of it, the contract talks can resume and take priority.

#2017. One Chris Davies quality at Birmingham City is 'beyond anything' Tom Huddlestone has seen

Birmingham City assistant coach and former Premier League midfielder, Tom Huddlestone, has issued a stunning claim on the coaching level of current St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park boss, Chris Davies.

Davies is now in his third season in charge of Blues, having taken over in June 2024 ahead of the club's solitary League One campaign this century.

His tenure hasn't been without mixed reviews, but right now the West Midlands outfit are fourth in the Championship, having lost for just a third time this campaign on Saturday against Stoke City in underwhelming fashion.

Davies' side will be hoping for a swift response when they face lowly Burnley on Wednesday night, with it representing another chance for the 41-year-old to show why he is so highly thought of.

Davies oversaw the most remarkable first season at St. Andrew's, with his side breaking the EFL's all-time points record en route back to the Championship, before finishing 10th last term.

There were some question marks over his future following a 6-1 EFL Cup loss to Brentford in August, but aside from three away losses to Norwich City, Cardiff City and Stoke, Birmingham have looked largely impressive across all departments.

Huddlestone, 39, became one of Davies' assistants in September 2024 after a brief spell at Wigan Athletic with former Hull City teammate, Shaun Maloney.

Asked how the game has changed from a coaching perspective by another ex-Hull colleague, Aaron McLean, and Adam Virgo, the former England international heaped praise on both head coaches.

"The detail that Shaun and Chris go into is beyond anything I've seen in terms of every little detail," Huddlestone revealed.

"Full clarity on how we press, how we play.

"A lot of stuff in my career, before Marco Silva (at Hull), was generally quite off-the-cuff, to be honest," he added.

"You'd have a system, know which three or four players you wanted to get the ball, but other than that, it was like 'go and do your thing'.

"The clarity is the biggest thing, and the details, even in throw-ins and every little detail in the game is nailed down and gone through."

There has certainly been shifts in how Birmingham have played in the early weeks of the current season compared to last, with an onus on moving the ball at speed and being direct when required.

Blues averaged 53.7% possession in last season's Championship, which was the seventh-highest total, but now have the ball just 51.6% of the time inside the first 16 matches.

Although there isn't much of a difference between such numbers, the way in which Birmingham have attacked has required far less time on the ball due to the speed of forwards such as Liam Millar and Patrick Roberts, as well as the intelligence of August Priske and Jay Stansfield.

Davies also admitted pre-season that more goals were required in order to sustain a promotion bid, and such feats have certainly been the case, whilst 15 concessions mean Blues have the joint-best defensive record along with Southampton.

The three West Midlands rivals' fixture schedule makes for an interesting night next month

Huddlestone played 242 times at Premier League level under some of the game's most experienced figures, such as Steve Bruce, Harry Redknapp and Glenn Hoddle.

The former technically gifted midfielder also knows what it takes to earn promotion from the Championship, having done so with Hull 10 years ago, and such knowledge will be pivotal for Davies and Birmingham's current squad to lean on.

Blues fans, can Chris Davies become an elite-level manager? Comment below!

Huddlestone is also currently coaching with Ben Futcher as part of the England Under-20s setup, and it may not be too long until we see him lead a team in the dugout, having recently stated: “I do want to try my hand at managing, but I’m not in no rush for that."

#2015. Liverpool v Manchester United: Five classic wins at Anfield

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

From Diego Forlan’s unforgettable double to Juan Mata’s spectacular volley, Manchester United have enjoyed some memorable victories at Anfield over the years.

With Mauricio Pochettino preparing for his first match in charge against Liverpool on Sunday, we look back at five occasions when United emerged victorious from one of English football’s fiercest fixtures.

United travelled to Anfield on 19 April 1997 knowing Liverpool could overtake them at the top of the Premier League with a victory. Instead, centre-back Gary Pallister scored twice with headers from David Beckham deliveries, either side of John Barnes’s equaliser.

Andy Cole added a third after the interval, heading home from Gary Neville’s cross following an error by Liverpool goalkeeper David James. The victory helped Sir Alex Ferguson’s side take a decisive step towards retaining the Premier League title, which they secured the following month.

Fresh from winning the Treble, United returned to Anfield for a remarkable match in September 1999. Jamie Carragher headed into his own net inside three minutes before Andy Cole doubled United’s advantage.

Sami Hyypia pulled a goal back for Liverpool, but Carragher’s second own goal restored the visitors’ two-goal lead. Patrik Berger reduced the deficit again, and United faced a difficult finish after Cole was sent off for a second bookable offence. Despite playing the closing stages with 10 men, Ferguson’s side held on for a 3-2 victory.

Diego Forlan had endured a frustrating start to his United career before producing a performance that supporters would remember for years. The Uruguayan opened the scoring after Liverpool goalkeeper Jerzy Dudek failed to gather the ball, allowing him to finish from close range.

Forlan added a second just minutes later, beating Dudek at his near post to put United in control. Sami Hyypia pulled one back for Liverpool, but Ferguson’s side held on for a victory that proved valuable in their successful pursuit of the 2002-03 Premier League title. Forlan’s double also inspired a terrace song that remains familiar to United supporters.

Few United victories at Anfield have been as dramatic as the one secured by John O’Shea in March 2007. Liverpool had enjoyed the better opportunities, and United’s task became harder when Paul Scholes was sent off late in the second half.

With a goalless draw appearing increasingly likely, Cristiano Ronaldo’s free-kick was parried by goalkeeper Pepe Reina. Substitute O’Shea reacted quickest, firing the rebound high into the net in stoppage time. The unexpected winner gave United three valuable points on their way to reclaiming the Premier League title.

Juan Mata produced one of the most memorable individual performances in this fixture during Louis van Gaal’s first season as United manager. The Spaniard opened the scoring after 14 minutes, finishing a move involving Ander Herrera, before doubling United’s lead with a spectacular scissor-kick volley after half-time.

Liverpool captain Steven Gerrard had been sent off just 38 seconds after coming on as a substitute at the interval. Daniel Sturridge reduced the deficit, while Wayne Rooney missed a late penalty, but United held on for a 2-1 victory. Mata’s second goal remains one of the outstanding moments of United’s Premier League visits to Anfield.

Those five victories underline the different ways United have succeeded at Anfield, from outstanding individual performances to late winners and determined defensive displays. Pochettino now has an opportunity to begin his managerial reign with a memorable result against United’s biggest rivals.

Which United victory at Anfield is your favourite? Were you there for Forlan’s double, O’Shea’s winner or Mata’s volley? Share your memories in the comments below.

#2013. Manchester United’s 34-goal defensive record shows scale of Pochettino’s task

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Manchester United have conceded more goals than any other club in Europe’s five major domestic leagues this season, underlining the scale of the challenge facing Mauricio Pochettino following Sunday’s 4-0 defeat at Liverpool.

Pochettino’s first match in charge ended in a heavy defeat at Anfield after defender Leny Yoro was sent off in the eighth minute. Although the dismissal left United facing an uphill struggle, their defensive record suggests the new head coach has inherited problems that extend well beyond one match.

According to an analysis published by the Premier League, United have conceded 34 goals in 16 matches across all competitions this season. That is the highest total of any club in Europe’s five major domestic leagues, with Sunday’s defeat adding four more goals to an already concerning record.

The Premier League also calculated Liverpool’s expected goals at 4.38, suggesting the hosts created enough chances to have won by an even greater margin. United’s difficulties began when Matthijs de Ligt misjudged a long ball, and Yoro fouled Alexander Isak as the Liverpool forward attempted to break through on goal.

Dominik Szoboszlai scored from the resulting free-kick, with Florian Wirtz doubling Liverpool’s advantage just three minutes later. Rio Ngumoha and substitute Victor Munoz completed the scoring after half-time.

The defeat left United 19th in the Premier League, with just eight points from their opening 11 matches and one league victory all season. Pochettino acknowledged the painful nature of the result but argued that the early dismissal had transformed the contest. He also questioned whether Yoro should have been sent off, given the circumstances of the challenge on Isak.

The Argentine’s first tactical plan was disrupted almost immediately, making it difficult to draw firm conclusions about how United might perform under him with a full complement of players.

Nevertheless, the defensive record accumulated before his arrival makes clear that improving the team’s organisation will be a central part of his work. The Premier League’s analysis highlighted De Ligt’s mistake before the sending-off and the opportunities Liverpool continued to create against United’s depleted defence.

United return to Champions League action on Wednesday when they travel to Portugal to face Sporting, giving Pochettino little time to address the issues exposed at Anfield. The fixture offers an early opportunity to assess how he intends to organise his defence and whether the team can respond to another damaging result.

Pochettino must also decide how to balance defensive stability with the need to improve results. United’s league position means they cannot afford to spend too long finding that balance. The circumstances of Sunday’s defeat were unusual, and the red card undoubtedly made United’s task more difficult. But conceding 34 goals in 16 matches points to a wider problem that will require sustained improvement rather than a reaction to one result.

For Pochettino, making United harder to beat is an obvious starting point. Wednesday’s trip to Sporting will provide his next opportunity to begin that process.

#2012. Chelsea Monitor Jarrod Bowen And Crysencio Summerville

Chelsea’s wide-player search has moved back into focus after the Guardian reported that the club are monitoring West Ham pair Jarrod Bowen and Crysencio Summerville.

The update lands during a busy early market for Xabi Alonso’s incoming rebuild.

The Guardian’s latest transfer round-up states that Chelsea like both West Ham forwards, while an earlier report noted Chelsea and Manchester United had tracked Summerville.

That same earlier update also said Aston Villa, Chelsea and Everton had expressed interest in Bowen.

West Ham pulling away at Stamford Bridge 👀Jarrod Bowen stuns Chelsea early before Crysencio Summerville doubles the lead. pic.twitter.com/Ktwr4pKZGH

Alonso officially begins work on July 1, with Chelsea confirming he has signed a four-year contract to lead the men’s team.

The winger search is not just about adding more attacking names. It is about identifying profiles who can quickly give Chelsea sharper final-third decisions.

Bowen would offer immediate Premier League output, penalty-box timing and a direct threat from the right.

Summerville, meanwhile, fits the younger, explosive one-v-one profile that would sit neatly inside Alonso’s longer-term wing plan.

Read Chelsea has already tracked the separate Summerville angle, but this latest dual link sharpens the picture.

Chelsea appear to be testing two different solutions to the same problem: proven reliability or higher-upside acceleration.

With pre-season closing in, that is exactly the sort of market check Alonso needs before deciding how much of Chelsea’s attacking rebuild should lean on speed, touchline width and more ruthless wide output.
